But they thought I should come in and talk to someone, so… here I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any recent weight loss that wasn’t on purpose, like not trying to lose weight but still dropping pounds?\n\nPatient: I mean… I didn’t really think of it as weight loss, exactly. The insurance physical doctor was the one who said my weight was too low. I guess to me I just looked lean, you know, from being on ladders all day and working long painting jobs. But… yeah, that’s why they told me to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any unintentional weight loss recently, or has your weight stayed about the same over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I honestly hadn’t been tracking it that closely. I thought my weight was just kind of… normal for me, maybe lean because I’m on ladders and working long days painting. The insurance physical doctor was the one who said it was too low, so I guess that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight without trying, like without dieting or exercising more, in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I was trying to lose weight, no. I didn’t really notice it as a problem myself—if anything I just figured I looked lean from being on ladders and working long days painting. The doctor at the insurance physical was the one who said my weight was too low, so… I guess that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you may have lost unintentionally over the past six months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure on a number, honestly. I wasn’t weighing myself regularly or anything. I just… I came in because the doctor doing my insurance physical said my weight was too low. To me, I guess I just thought I looked lean from being on ladders and working long painting days, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 10% of your body weight without trying in the past six months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I could say it was more than 10%, honestly. I wasn’t really tracking it like that. The main thing is the insurance physical doctor said my weight was too low, but to me I just thought I looked lean from being on ladders and working long days painting. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The doctor at the insurance physical was the one who said my weight was too low, so… I guess that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you may have lost unintentionally over the past six months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure on a number, honestly. I wasn’t weighing myself regularly or anything. I just… I came in because the doctor doing my insurance physical said my weight was too low. To me, I guess I just thought I looked lean from being on ladders and working long painting days, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 10% of your body weight without trying in the past six months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I could say it was more than 10%, honestly. I wasn’t really tracking it like that. The main thing is the insurance physical doctor said my weight was too low, but to me I just thought I looked lean from being on ladders and working long days painting. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately what your current weight and height are?\n\nPatient: I don’t have an exact number, sorry. I guess that’s part of why I’m here—the doctor at the insurance physical said my weight was too low. They just said my weight was too low, which kind of surprised me. I guess I’ve been thinking I just look lean from being on ladders and working long painting days. I know that’s not ideal. The reason I came in is because the doctor doing my insurance physical said my weight was too low. I guess I’ve been seeing it more as me just being lean from painting work—being on ladders and working long days.
